## 2 About This Document
## 2.1 General Statements
## Purpose
This document is intended to acquaint readers with:
●  The technical principles of features and their related parameters
●  The scenarios where these features are used, the benefits they provide, and
the impact they have on networks and functions
●  Requirements that must be met before feature activation
●  Parameter configuration required for feature activation, verification of feature
activation, and monitoring of feature performance
## NO TE
This document only provides guidance for feature activation. Feature deployment and
feature gains depend on the specifics of the network scenario where the feature is
deployed. To achieve optimal gains, contact Huawei professional service engineers.
Functions mentioned in this document work properly only when enabled in the
specified applicable scenarios (such as RAT and networking). If a function not
mentioned in this document is enabled or a function is enabled in a scenario not
specified as applicable, exceptions or other impacts may occur.

document apply only to the corresponding software release. For future software
releases, refer to the corresponding updated product documentation.

## 3 General Principles
3.1 Basic Concepts and Definitions
The LTE system uses shared-channel data transmissions where time-frequency
resources are dynamically shared among UEs. eNodeBs perform scheduling to
allocate time-frequency resources for uplink and downlink transmissions.
## Schedulers
LTE schedulers are located at the Media Access Control (MAC) layer. The basic
function of a scheduler is to allocate appropriate system resources to UEs in each
subframe for data transmission and reception.
REs and RBs
Resource elements (REs) are the smallest time-frequency resource units in the LTE
system. An RE occupies one subcarrier in the frequency domain and one
orthogonal frequency division multiplexing (OFDM) symbol in the time domain. A
resource block (RB) includes 12 subcarriers in the frequency domain and lasts for
0.5 ms in the time domain. An RB pair forms the minimum scheduling unit,
comprising 12 subcarriers in the frequency domain and one subframe (lasting for
1 ms) in the time domain. Figure 3-1 shows an RB pair.
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.9

Figure 3-1 RB pair
A UE can be allocated multiple RBs in each transmission time interval (TTI). The
maximum number of available RBs depends on the system bandwidth, as listed in
## Table 3-1.
Table 3-1 Number of available RBs per system bandwidth
System Bandwidth (MHz)
Number of RBs
## 1.46
## 315
## 525
## 1050
## 1575
## 20100

## MCS
Modulation and coding schemes (MCSs) are used by an eNodeB to ensure the
transmission quality and efficiency of UE services. If the channel quality is good,
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.10

an eNodeB selects an MCS with a more efficient modulation scheme and a higher
code rate (with fewer redundant bits inserted). If the channel quality is poor, the
eNodeB selects one with a less efficient modulation scheme and a lower code rate
(with more redundant bits inserted).
MCSs are selected during scheduling. Each MCS represents a specific combination
of a modulation scheme and a channel code rate. The modulation schemes
include quadrature phase shift keying (QPSK), 16 quadrature amplitude
modulation (16QAM), 64QAM, and 256QAM. Among them:
●  QPSK modulates two information bits into each modulation symbol.
●  16QAM modulates four information bits into each modulation symbol.
●  64QAM modulates six information bits into each modulation symbol.
●  256QAM modulates eight information bits into each modulation symbol.
Channel code rates, which are typically less than 1, indicate the proportion of valid
information bits among transmitted bits after redundant bits have been inserted.
The scheduler provides a high-order modulation scheme and a high code rate
when channel conditions are favorable. The higher the modulation order and code
rate, the higher the transmission efficiency.
3.2 Basic Procedure of Uplink Scheduling
Scheduling is the procedure where the scheduler allocates RBs and selects MCSs
for UEs. Figure 3-2 shows the basic procedure of uplink scheduling. The inputs to
the scheduler include the results of physical-layer measurement and reporting,
MAC protocol data unit (PDU) reception processing, and upper-layer configuration
and UE capability information processing.
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.11

Figure 3-2 Uplink scheduling procedure
Physical-Layer Measurement and Reporting
Physical-layer measurement and reporting involve uplink signal to interference
plus noise ratio (SINR) measurement, uplink interference measurement, cyclic
redundancy check (CRC), and physical uplink shared channel (PUSCH) DTX
detection.
●  Uplink SINR measurement
Uplink SINRs directly reflect the uplink channel quality of UEs. The eNodeB
measures sounding reference signals (SRSs) or demodulation reference signals
(DMRSs) to obtain SINRs.
●  Uplink interference measurement
Uplink interference reflects the amount of interference there is across the
entire bandwidth of a cell. The scheduler considers the interference when it
allocates RBs to UEs.
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.12

## ●  CRC
Uplink data is correctly received only if the CRC results are correct.
●  PUSCH DTX detection
If the CRC fails and DTX is detected on the PUSCH, uplink data is not sent
because no uplink scheduling indication from the eNodeB is received on the
UE side. It is not the case that the UE has sent uplink data but data
demodulation at the eNodeB fails.
MAC PDU Reception
MAC PDU reception includes data reception and MAC control element (CE)
reception.
●  After the scheduler receives uplink data from a UE, the scheduler calculates
and updates the UE data rate, which is used subsequently to calculate the
scheduling priority for the UE.
●  The MAC CEs of a UE mainly include:
–Buffer status reports (BSRs). A UE sends BSRs to the eNodeB to indicate
how much data is in the uplink buffer of the UE. The scheduler allocates
uplink resources to the UE based on the size of this buffer.
–   Power headroom reports (PHRs). A PHR reflects the power status of a UE.
Power headroom is the difference between the maximum allowed
transmit (TX) power and the TX power currently being used in the uplink.
Based on the UE power headroom, the scheduler works with uplink
power control to maintain an appropriate power spectral density for
uplink signals. For details about uplink power control, see
## Power Control.
Upper-Layer Configuration and UE Capability
Upper-layer configuration mainly refers to the quality of service (QoS)
requirements of UE services. The QoS information about an LTE bearer includes:
●  A QoS class identifier (QCI)
●  A guaranteed bit rate (GBR) of GBR services
●  A maximum bit rate (MBR) of GBR services
●  An aggregate maximum bit rate (AMBR) of non-GBR services
For details on standardized QCIs, see 3GPP TS 23.203. Huawei eNodeBs also
support extended QCIs. For details, see
QoS Management.
During scheduling, the scheduler considers UE capabilities, for example, the
maximum number of bits that a UE can transmit in each TTI.
Processing by the Scheduler
In uplink scheduling, after the scheduler obtains required inputs, it first checks
uplink scheduling resources and then performs different types of scheduling in
descending order of scheduling priority: semi-persistent scheduling, HARQ
retransmission scheduling, and scheduling of initial transmission services.
●  For details on semi-persistent scheduling, see
VoLTE.
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.13

●  For details on HARQ retransmission, see 3.2.2 Uplink HARQ.
●  For details on scheduling of initial transmission services, see 3.2.3 Basic
## Uplink Scheduling Policies.
In FDD, when scheduling is enabled together with Physical Resource Slicing
(controlled by the PHY_RES_SLICING_SWITCH option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
OperatorFunSwitch parameter), the eNodeB performs
scheduling based on PRB groups. However, the eNodeB complies with the original
processes and does not differentiate PRB groups in semi-persistent scheduling,
control message service scheduling, and HARQ retransmission service scheduling.
For details about Physical Resource Slicing, see
Physical Resource Slicing (FDD).
## 3.2.1 Uplink Scheduling Triggering
In the uplink, it is UEs that transmit data. Therefore, the eNodeB needs to know
the uplink buffer status of UEs before it can allocate resources for uplink
scheduling. The procedure for triggering uplink scheduling is as follows:
-  Before transmitting data, a UE sends the eNodeB an SR using the scheduling
request indicator (SRI) on the physical uplink control channel (PUCCH) to
request uplink resources for data transmission.
-  Upon receiving the SR, the eNodeB responds to the SR and schedules the UE.
(This type of UE is referred to as an SR UE in this document.)
-  The SR UE uses the uplink resources allocated by the eNodeB to transmit
MAC PDUs, including BSRs.
-  If the amount of data indicated by a BSR is greater than 0, the eNodeB
continues scheduling the UE. Otherwise, the eNodeB stops the uplink
scheduling of the UE.
3.2.2 Uplink HARQ
## Overview
Synchronous HARQ is used for uplink retransmissions. In this HARQ scheme, the
UE sends uplink data
k TTIs after the eNodeB delivers an uplink grant for the
initial transmission. After i TTIs, the eNodeB sends HARQ feedback to the initial
transmission and then determines whether the UE needs to perform a
retransmission. In FDD, both
i and k are fixed to 4.
Uplink HARQ Retransmission Procedure
There are two types of uplink synchronous HARQ retransmissions: non-adaptive
and adaptive. The differences are as follows:
●  In non-adaptive HARQ, the number of RBs and the MCS used for
retransmissions are identical to those for the initial transmission. If the
positions of RBs for a retransmission collide with those of uplink resources,
such as physical random access channel (PRACH) and PUCCH resources, the
eNodeB re-selects RBs at appropriate positions. If the RB reselection fails, the
eNodeB suspends the retransmission.
●  In adaptive HARQ, the eNodeB adaptively adjusts the number of RBs, their
positions, and the MCS for retransmissions. In this way, uplink resources are
more quickly allocated for uplink retransmissions.
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.14

Huawei eNodeBs support both adaptive and non-adaptive HARQ in the uplink and
automatic switching between the adaptive and non-adaptive schemes. The uplink
HARQ retransmission scheme can be set using the
CellUlschAlgo.AdaptHarqSwitch parameter. Figure 3-3 shows the uplink HARQ
retransmission procedure.
Figure 3-3 Uplink HARQ retransmission procedure
Uplink HARQ retransmissions for an initial retransmission are stopped when the
number of uplink HARQ retransmissions reaches a maximum value, which is
specified by the CellUlschAlgo.
UlHarqMaxTxNum parameter.
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.15

## 3.2.3 Basic Uplink Scheduling Policies
## Basic Uplink Scheduling Algorithms
Four basic uplink scheduling algorithms are available: maximum carrier-to-
interference ratio (Max C/I), round robin (RR), proportional fair (PF), and
enhanced proportional fair (EPF).
The four scheduling algorithms consider different factors in calculating UE
priorities. Details are as follows:
●  Max C/I
The Max C/I algorithm takes only channel quality into account when
allocating air interface resources and schedules only UEs with the best
channel quality at that moment. This algorithm maximizes system
throughput. However, it cannot ensure that UEs in the same cell are
scheduled equally because the UEs in a cell do not all experience the same
channel conditions. A UE may never be scheduled if it is constantly
experiencing poor channel conditions. As a result, user experience is poor. This
scheduling algorithm does not consider QoS for UE services.
## ●  RR
The RR algorithm allows UEs to take turns using shared resources. RR ensures
that UE scheduling is fairer than when Max C/I is used. However, this fairness
is at the expense of system throughput. This scheduling algorithm does not
consider QoS for UE services.
## ●  PF
The PF algorithm is a tradeoff between Max C/I and RR. This algorithm takes
the scheduling fairness, channel quality, and the historical number of
transmitted bits into consideration. However, this algorithm does not take
QoS requirements into consideration and therefore cannot ensure satisfactory
user experience.
## ●  EPF
The EPF algorithm is an enhanced version of the PF algorithm. Compared
with PF, EPF considers user experience and guarantees QoS of services. In
addition, Huawei adds six capacity factors for scheduling priority selection
oriented towards capacity or resource fairness in EPF.
Figure 3-4 shows the relationships between the four scheduling algorithms.
Figure 3-4 Relationships between the scheduling algorithms
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.16

An operator can select an appropriate scheduling algorithm by setting the
CellUlschAlgo.UlschStrategy parameter. In general, the EPF algorithm is
recommended as the basic uplink scheduling policy.
Scheduling of Initial Transmission Services
When scheduling initial transmission services, the scheduler performs the
following steps:
-  Selects UEs.
The scheduler selects or does not select UEs meeting certain conditions. For
details, see 6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling.
-  Calculates priorities and manages scheduling queues.
During multi-UE scheduling, the scheduler determines which UEs to schedule
within the specific TTI based on UE priorities. The methods used to calculate
the UE priorities depend on the services. For priority descriptions, see 7 UE
Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling.
-  Determines the amount of data to be scheduled for the UEs.
The scheduler triggers uplink scheduling and determines the amount of data
to be scheduled based on UE-reported information. For details on the
triggering of uplink scheduling, see 3.2.1 Uplink Scheduling Triggering. For
details on the calculation of the amount of data to be scheduled, see 8 UE
Data Amount Calculation for Uplink Scheduling.
-  Determines the number of RBs to allocate.
The scheduler estimates the number of uplink RBs required by a UE in a TTI
based on the amount of data to be scheduled in the TTI, number of unused
RBs, power headroom status, token bucket status for QoS guarantee, and
maximum number of RBs supported by a single carrier. For details, see 9 RB
Quantity Calculation for Uplink Scheduling.
-  Allocates RB resources.
The scheduler determines which RBs, among the remaining RBs, to allocate
based on a number of factors. For details, see 10 RB Allocation for Uplink
Scheduling. In addition, Huawei eNodeBs support frequency hopping in
accordance with 3GPP specifications. The CellUlschAlgo.
UlHoppingType
parameter is used to configure frequency hopping. However, frequency
hopping has a negative impact on network performance and therefore is not
recommended for use.
-  Selects MCSs.
The scheduler selects an MCS for a UE mainly based on the uplink SINR. For
details, see 11 MCS Selection for Uplink Scheduling.
eRAN
## Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description3 General Principles
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.17

## 4 Uplink Scheduling Resource Acquisition
Before the scheduler starts uplink scheduling, it needs to obtain available uplink
resources, which mainly refer to PUSCH RB resources. This section describes
variations of available PUSCH RBs with scenarios as well as the variations caused
by manual blocking of PUSCH RBs.
4.1 Calculation of Available PUSCH RBs
## 4.1.1 Principles
In the uplink of an LTE system, the PUSCH, PUCCH, and PRACH share the entire
uplink bandwidth.
●  The PUCCH occupies RBs at two ends of a cell bandwidth, and the number of
RBs is dependent on the number of UEs in semi-persistent scheduling mode
and the amount of resources required to carry information on the PUCCH.
The information includes HARQ feedback to downlink dynamic scheduling,
CQIs indicating downlink channel quality, and SRIs. For details on the PUCCH,
see
## Physical Channel Resource Management.
●  The PRACH occupies six RBs in the uplink.
●  The number of PUSCH RBs allocated to a UE is
, where , ,
and  are not minus integers.
Available PUSCH RBs vary as follows:
●  Generally, the number of available PUSCH RBs is equal to the number of RBs
across the entire channel bandwidth minus the number of RBs occupied by
the PUCCH and PRACH.
●  If the PrachRbReuseSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch
parameter is selected, the PUSCH can occupy PRACH RBs.
●  When the PuschUsePucchRbSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is selected:
–   If the cell is serving only one UE, the PUSCH can occupy PUCCH RBs.
–   If the cell is serving more than one UE, the PUSCH cannot occupy PUCCH
RBs.
eRAN

## 4.1.2 Network Analysis
## 4.1.2.1 Benefits
A rise in the number of available PUSCH RBs increases uplink UE peak throughput.

## Function Impacts.
If the PUSCH occupies unused RBs of the PUCCH, selecting the
PuschUsePucchRbSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch parameter
may decrease the PRB usage.

4.2 Manual Blocking of PUSCH RBs
## 4.2.1 Principles
If certain PUSCH RBs experience strong interference from a specific external source
or are unavailable because of spectrum planning, these RBs can be manually
blocked so that the eNodeB will not schedule them in the uplink.
The parameters in the CellRbReserve MO determine the RB blocking mode and
the positions and quantity of these RBs. This RB blocking function does not take
effect on control channels or common channels. Therefore, it is recommended that
the blocked PUSCH RBs not overlap the RBs occupied by the PUCCH and PRACH.
## 4.2.2 Network Analysis
## 4.2.2.1 Benefits
## None
## 4.2.2.2 Impacts

## Function Impacts.
The function has no impact on the network.

5 Optimization of Uplink HARQ
## Retransmissions
5.1 Retransmission Upon Detection of PUSCH DTX
## 5.1.1 Principles
Generally, the result of PUSCH data reception by the eNodeB is ACK or NACK.
Given that NACK is possibly caused by no data transmission from the UE side,
PUSCH DTX detection can be enabled on the eNodeB to detect such situation and
then the result of PUSCH data reception can be ACK, NACK, or DTX. In FDD, the
PUSCH DTX detection function is controlled by the PuschDtxSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter.
●  If the result of PUSCH data reception by the eNodeB is ACK, the data
transmission ends.
●  If the result of PUSCH data reception by the eNodeB is NACK, the UE is
instructed to perform data retransmission.
●  If the result of PUSCH data reception by the eNodeB is DTX, uplink SINRs are
not adjusted subsequently.
In FDD, the CellUlschAlgo.
PuschDtxSchStrategy parameter determines the
HARQ process scheduling policy for a data packet.
–   If this parameter is set to NEW_TX, the eNodeB stops data retransmission
for the HARQ process and delivers UE initial-transmission scheduling
information on the PDCCH.
–   If this parameter is set to ADAPTIVE_RETX, the eNodeB adopts adaptive
HARQ retransmission scheduling for the HARQ process to deliver the
same uplink scheduling information over the PDCCH as that in the initial
transmission, including the indicated MCS and number of RBs. If DRX has
been activated, adaptive HARQ retransmission scheduling is performed
only when the On Duration Timer specified by the
DrxParaGroup.
OnDurationTimer parameter is running.
–   If this parameter is set to EN_ADAPTIVE_RETX and DRX is not activated,
the eNodeB uses adaptive HARQ retransmission scheduling for the HARQ
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Optimization of Uplink HARQ Retransmissions
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.28

process to deliver the same uplink scheduling information over the
PDCCH as that in the initial transmission, including the indicated MCS
and number of RBs. However, if DRX has been activated, adaptive HARQ
retransmission scheduling is performed only during the active time of the
## UE.
## 5.1.2 Network Analysis
## 5.1.2.1 Benefits
When PUSCH DTX detection is enabled, the uplink scheduler stops invalid
retransmissions and performs initial transmissions based on the PUSCH DTX
detection result, saving uplink resources and increasing Cell Uplink Average
Throughput and average uplink user-perceived throughput. (Average uplink user-
perceived throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/
L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)
## 5.1.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 5.1.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
The function has no impact on the network.

5.2 Uplink HARQ Retransmission Optimization
## 5.2.1 Principles
If consecutive CRC errors occur in a HARQ retransmission process, a reduced code
rate can be used for HARQ retransmissions when the cell serves less than 150
RRC_CONNECTED UEs and idle RBs are available. The TBS and MCS used for
HARQ retransmissions are the same as those used for the initial transmission.
Therefore, more RBs need to be allocated for HARQ retransmissions.
The UlLast2RetransSchOptSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch
parameter specifies whether uplink HARQ retransmissions use a reduced code
rate. When this option is selected, a reduced code rate is used for the last two
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description5 Optimization of Uplink HARQ Retransmissions
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.31

uplink HARQ retransmissions and more RBs are allocated to the two
retransmissions.
In adaptive uplink HARQ retransmissions for voice services, the MCS selection
policy is specified by the UlVoipRblerControlSwitch option of the
CellUlschAlgo.UlEnhencedVoipSchSw parameter.
●  If this option is selected, the scheduler selects lower-order MCSs for voice
services than for data services.
●  If this option is deselected, the scheduler uses the same policy to select the
MCSs for voice and data services.
If the UlLast2RetransSchOptSwitch and UlVoipRblerControlSwitch options are
both selected, the eNodeB increases the number of RBs and reduces the code rate
also for voice services in the last two HARQ retransmissions. The eNodeB increases
the number of RBs for the retransmissions even if the retransmissions conflict with
measurement gaps.
## 5.2.2 Network Analysis
## 5.2.2.1 Benefits
Optimization of uplink HARQ retransmissions reduces the uplink RBLER and
## Service Drop Rate.
## 5.2.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 5.2.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
Optimization of uplink HARQ retransmissions increases the number of uplink RBs
used for retransmissions and therefore may slightly reduce Cell Uplink Average
## Throughput.
## 5.2.3 Requirements
## 5.2.3.1 Licenses
There are no license requirements for basic functions.

6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
For details on the basic policies used in UE selection for uplink scheduling, see
3.2.3 Basic Uplink Scheduling Policies. The scheduler acts differently in UE
selection for some special scenarios. The UE selection functions used in special
scenarios include the following:
●  Uplink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs
●  SRI detection optimization
●  PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization
●  Proactive scheduling for uplink signaling
●  Optimization on false SR detection in DRX mode
## ●  Preallocation
●  Outer-loop DSCP-based preallocation
●  Scheduling restriction on abnormal UEs
●  Preallocation for NSA DC UEs (FDD)
●  Uplink short-interval SPS (FDD)
6.1 Uplink Scheduling Termination for Abnormal UEs
## 6.1.1 Principles
Termination of the uplink scheduling of UEs in extremely weak coverage areas or
with radio link failures prevents these UEs from overusing system resources. When
the AbnUeSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
CellSchStrategySwitch
parameter is selected, the uplink scheduler of the eNodeB determines whether the
UE link is abnormal based on uplink demodulation performance. Specifically, if the
number of successive times that the uplink CRC for a UE fails exceeds the value of
the CellUlschAlgo.
UlSchAbnUeThd parameter, the uplink scheduler determines
that demodulation performance is poor and the UE link is abnormal. In this
situation, the eNodeB clears the maintained buffer size settings of the UE to stop
uplink scheduling for the UE.
The eNodeB attempts to schedule a UE if it receives two consecutive SRs from the
UE. If the CRC for the UE succeeds, the uplink scheduler clears the abnormal state
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.35

of the UE link and the UE can be scheduled normally. If the CRC fails, the UE link
continues to be in the abnormal state.
With the function of forbidding "scheduling termination for abnormal UEs" for
voice service UEs enabled, "uplink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs" does
not take effect for voice service UEs. For details about the function of forbidding
"scheduling termination for abnormal UEs" for voice service UEs, see VoLTE.
## 6.1.2 Network Analysis
## 6.1.2.1 Benefits
Uplink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs (those with links in the abnormal
state) saves system resources, increasing the Cell Uplink Average Throughput
and decreasing the uplink RBLER in case of resource insufficiency.
## 6.1.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 6.1.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
Uplink scheduling termination for abnormal UEs reduces the scheduling
opportunities and increases the scheduling delay for UEs under weak coverage.
This causes the average uplink UE throughput to decrease and the service drop
rate to increase for these UEs.

6.2 SRI Detection Optimization
## 6.2.1 Principles
When no data is transmitted in the uplink, there is a low probability of false SRI
detection that causes the eNodeB to maintain incorrect information about UE DRX
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.38

state. In this case, the CellUlschAlgo.SriFalseDetThdSwitch parameter can be set
to ON to increase the SRI false detection threshold for UEs in DRX state, reducing
the probability of false SRI detection.
Inaccurate measurements of interference and noise on the PUCCH possibly cause
the probability of SRI false detection to rise. The SriDetectEnhanceSW option of
the CellPucchAlgo.SriAlgoSwitch parameter can be selected to improve the
accuracy of such measurements and therefore reduce the probability of SRI false
detection.
## 6.2.2 Network Analysis
## 6.2.2.1 Benefits
SRI detection optimization reduces the probability of false SRI detection and
therefore decreases the uplink RBLER.
Uplink RBLER = (L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Rbler + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Rbler)/(L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.TB)
## 6.2.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 6.2.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
The function has no impact on the network.

6.3 PDCCH and PUSCH Resource Allocation
## Optimization
## 6.3.1 Principles
The number of UEs scheduled per TTI in the uplink is limited in a cell if the cell
serves a large number of RRC_CONNECTED UEs, a high percentage of the UEs is
running small-packet services or located at the cell edge, and PDCCH resources
are insufficient.
In this situation, PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization can be used.
With this function, the scheduler randomly selects a UE engaged in large-packet
data transmission at the cell center and reserves PDCCH resources for the UE to
ensure that the selected UE can be scheduled, improving the uplink RB usage and
uplink traffic volume. Figure 6-1 illustrates the principles for PDCCH and PUSCH
resource allocation optimization.
Figure 6-1 Principles for PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization
This optimization involves the following parameters:
●CellUlschAlgo.DataThdInPdcchPuschBal. This parameter specifies the data
volume threshold for determining UEs with large-packet data transmission.
●CellUlschAlgo.UeNumThdInPdcchPuschBal. This parameter specifies the UE
number threshold for triggering this optimization in a cell.
Note the following when using this optimization:
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.42

●  In RAN sharing scenarios, this optimization is not recommended because
there is a probability that the operator-specific resource occupation ratio is
not strictly applied due to random selection of a UE with large-packet data
transmission.
●  It is good practice to select the SchedulerCtrlPowerSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch parameter when using this optimization,
preventing UEs with large-packet data transmission from occupying excess
resources.
## 6.3.2 Network Analysis
## 6.3.2.1 Benefits
PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization increases the uplink RB usage
and therefore increases Cell Uplink Average Throughput.

The function has no impact on the network.

## 6.3.4.4 Activation Verification
-  Check that the cell meets the following basic requirements for enabling
PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization.
–   Average number of UEs in the cell (L.Traffic.User.Avg) > Value of the
CellUlschAlgo.UeNumThdInPdcchPuschBal parameter
–   CCE usage > 70%, where CCE usage = (L.ChMeas.CCE.CommUsed +
L.ChMeas.CCE.ULUsed + L.ChMeas.CCE.DLUsed)/L.ChMeas.CCE.Avail
–   Uplink RB usage < 80%, where Uplink RB usage =
L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.Used.Avg/L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.Avail
-  Compare the uplink RB usage within the same period of service time before
and after PDCCH and PUSCH resource allocation optimization is enabled. If
the uplink RB usage increases, this function has taken effect.
## 6.3.4.5 Network Monitoring
For details, see 6.3.2 Network Analysis.
6.4 Optimization on False SR Detection in DRX Mode
## 6.4.1 Principles
If an eNodeB falsely detects an SR from a UE (which actually does not send the
SR) and the UE is not in the DRX state, the eNodeB sends an uplink grant. Upon
reception of the uplink grant, the UE sends data or padding as a response
regardless of whether there is a service waiting for data transmission. The eNodeB
monitors the PUSCH to receive data and then responds with ACK or NACK. If the
eNodeB falsely detects an SR from a UE in the DRX state, the eNodeB starts SR
pending for the UE as stipulated in 3GPP specifications and sends an uplink grant
to the UE. In addition, if there is data in the downlink buffer for the UE, the
eNodeB also sends a downlink grant to the UE. However, the UE is in a sleep
period, leading to DTX. Therefore, in DRX scenarios, the
CellDrxPara.
DrxSrDetectOptSwitch parameter can be set to ON to reduce issues
caused by the inconsistency between the eNodeB-maintained UE DRX state and
the actual UE DRX state as a result of false SR detection.
## 6.4.2 Network Analysis
## 6.4.2.1 Benefits
Optimization of false SR detection in DRX mode reduces the uplink RBLER and
downlink RBLER.
●  Uplink RBLER = (L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Rbler + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Rbler
+ L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Rbler)/(L.Traffic.UL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.UL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.UL.SCH.256QAM.TB)
●  Downlink RBLER = (L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.ErrTB.Rbler +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.ErrTB.Rbler + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.ErrTB.Rbler
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.46

+ L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.ErrTB.Rbler)/(L.Traffic.DL.SCH.QPSK.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.16QAM.TB + L.Traffic.DL.SCH.64QAM.TB +
L.Traffic.DL.SCH.256QAM.TB)

## 6.5 Preallocation
## 6.5.1 Principles
During uplink preallocation, an eNodeB proactively sends uplink scheduling
indications to UEs. Uplink preallocation takes effect only when the
CellUlschAlgo.
UlschStrategy parameter is set to ULSCH_STRATEGY_EPF.
Preallocation is also affected by the number of RRC_CONNECTED UEs in the cell.
This function takes effect only when the number of RRC_CONNECTED UEs in the
cell does not exceed the value of the CellUlschAlgo.
PreallocationUserCntThld
parameter.
Preallocation can work in the following modes:
●  Normal mode
●  Smart mode
There are cell- and QCI-level preallocation parameter settings. If available, the
eNodeB preferentially selects the preallocation mode based on QCI-level
preallocation configuration.
Figure 6-2 shows the relationship between the preallocation modes.
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.49

Figure 6-2 Relationship between the preallocation modes
To reduce the number of RBs occupied by preallocation, the amount of bandwidth
made available for uplink preallocation can be adapted. This function is controlled
by the CellUlschAlgo.
PrealloSystemBwCoeff parameter. The value of this
parameter ranges from 0 to 10. If this parameter is set to 0, this function is
disabled. If this parameter is set to a value other than 0, the number of RBs
available for uplink preallocation decreases. The larger the parameter value, the
smaller the amount of the decrease.
## Normal Preallocation
Normal preallocation is controlled by the PreAllocationSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter.
Normal preallocation is not subject to the setting of preallocation duration. In
FDD, normal preallocation can be performed within the On Duration period in
DRX mode only when CellDrxPara.
DrxOdPreSchSwitch is set to ON. Other
preallocation modes can take effect only after the option for normal preallocation,
PreAllocationSwitch, is selected. In addition, cell- and QCI-level preallocation
configurations can only take effect if this option is selected.
## Smart Preallocation
Uplink smart preallocation is controlled by the SmartPreAllocationSwitch option
of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter.
Smart preallocation is triggered by downlink services. It cannot be performed
before uplink-initiated TCP services such as web browsing start.
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.50

The smart preallocation duration is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocationDuration parameter, and smart preallocation
can be performed in DRX.
The preallocation duration for sparse packets (that is, ping packets) is determined
by the value of either the CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocationDuration parameter
or the CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse parameter, whichever is
larger.
●  Smart preallocation is disabled if both the
CellUlschAlgo.
SmartPreAllocationDuration and
CellUlschAlgo.
SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse parameters are set to 0.
●  Larger values of the CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocationDuration and
CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse parameters result in higher UE
power consumption.
## NO TE
Currently, preallocation of sparse-packet services only works for ping services.
To balance throughput and UE power consumption while ensuring that ping
services accurately reflect the packet round-trip delay on live networks, it is
recommended that the CellUlschAlgo.
SmartPreAllocationDuration parameter be
set to a small value (such as 50 ms) and the
CellUlschAlgo.SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse parameter be set to a value greater
than the interval between ping services (such as 1500 ms).
To shorten the smart preallocation duration, adaptation of the uplink smart
preallocation duration can be enabled. This function is controlled by the
CellUlschAlgo.
SmartPrealloDurationCoeff parameter. The value range of this
parameter is 0 to 10. The value 0 indicates that this function is disabled. If this
parameter is set to a value other than 0, the smart preallocation duration is
shortened.
## Cell-level Preallocation Configurations
The total resources made available for preallocation in a cell are specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationBandwidthRatio parameter. This parameter
determines the maximum proportion of the total RBs available for preallocation in
a TTI to the system bandwidth.
The amount of data that can be preallocated resources for a UE in a preallocation
queue is specified by the CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationSize parameter.
The amount of data that can be preallocated resources in the smart preallocation
for a UE depends on if the UE is accessing common or sparse-packet services.
●  For common services, the amount is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationSize parameter.
●  For sparse-packet services, the amount is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.PreallocationSizeForSparse parameter.
If preallocation is enabled and PUSCH RB resources are still available after UEs
with unsatisfied GBR, UEs with minimum GBR unfulfilled, and UEs with minimum
GBR fulfilled but AMBR unfulfilled are scheduled, then UEs with AMBR unfulfilled
are placed in the preallocation queue when the following conditions are met:
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.51

●  The UEs are not scheduled within the current TTI.
●  The UEs meet the requirement for the minimum interval between
preallocations.
The minimum interval between preallocations is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationMinPeriod parameter.
If smart preallocation is enabled, the minimum interval between
preallocations for sparse-packet services is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo
.PreAllocMinPeriodForSparse parameter. If this parameter is
set to SAME_AS_OTHER_SERVICE, the minimum interval between
preallocations is determined by the setting of the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationMinPeriod parameter.
●  The UE has not undergone semi-persistent scheduling.
●  The UE has a preallocation weight greater than 0.
The preallocation weight is specified by the QciPara.PreAllocationWeight
parameter, which is QCI-specific. The preallocation weight of a UE is
determined by the preallocation weight of the services with the highest-
priority QCI.
The resource preallocation priorities of UEs in the preallocation queue are
determined by the number of preallocations and the preallocation weight. A UE
with fewer preallocations and a greater preallocation weight has a higher priority.
If UEs have the same priority and preallocation weight, the eNodeB selects UEs for
preallocation at random.
QCI-level Preallocation Configurations
In QCI-level preallocation, preallocation parameter groups are configured on a per
QCI basis. A preallocation parameter group includes the configurations of the
preallocation mode, data volume, minimum preallocation interval, and
preallocation duration. Figure 6-3 shows the association between QCIs and
preallocation parameter groups. Each QCI associates with a maximum of one
preallocation parameter group.
Figure 6-3 QCI-level preallocation configurations
The eNodeB traverses the QCIs of all bearers that have been established on a UE.
●  If one or more QCIs are configured with valid PreallocationParaGroupId
values:
The eNodeB preferentially uses QCI-level preallocation configurations for the
## UE.
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.52

If multiple QCIs are configured with valid PreallocationParaGroupId values,
the eNodeB selects preallocation configurations for the UE based on a
minimum delay principle. The rules followed are as follows:
–   Rule of preallocation mode selection
The eNodeB preferentially uses the configurations for normal
preallocation. If the configurations for normal preallocation are
unavailable, the eNodeB uses the configurations for smart preallocation.
–   Rule of parameter selection in normal preallocation
If multiple sets of normal preallocation configurations are available, the
minimum preallocation interval equals the minimum one among the
configurations and the data amount for preallocation equals the
maximum one among the configurations.
–   Rule of parameter selection in smart preallocation
If multiple sets of smart preallocation configurations are available, the
minimum preallocation interval equals the minimum one among the
configurations, the data amount for preallocation equals the maximum
one among the configurations, and the smart preallocation duration
equals the maximum one among the configurations.
●  If none of QCIs is configured with a valid value of PreallocationParaGroupId:
The eNodeB uses cell-level preallocation configurations for the UE.
## 6.5.2 Network Analysis
## 6.5.2.1 Benefits
●  By increasing the number of times the eNodeB proactively schedules UEs,
uplink normal preallocation or uplink smart preallocation reduces the uplink
packet buffering time in the UEs, increases the response speed for UE services,
and improves the service experience.
●  Normal or smart preallocation in the uplink allows the eNodeB to prevent E-
UTRAN radio access bearer (E-RAB) setup signaling reception failures caused
by missing detection of SRs sent from the UEs. That is, normal or smart
preallocation helps increase the E-RAB Setup Success Rate.
●  Adaptation of the uplink smart preallocation duration results in a decreased
proportion of padding for smart preallocation, which increases average uplink
user-perceived throughput. (Average uplink user-perceived throughput =
L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)
## 6.5.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 6.5.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
The function has no impact on the network.

6.5.4.2 Using MML Commands
Before using MML commands, refer to 6.5.3 Requirements and complete the
parameter configurations for related functions based on the impact, dependency,
and mutually exclusive relationships between the functions, as well as the actual
network scenario. In addition, ensure that the license, hardware, networking, and
cell requirements are met.
Before using MML commands, confirm the related parameter modification

Modification" and "Caution" for the corresponding parameters in the parameter
reference.
## Activation Command Examples
//Setting the uplink scheduling policy to EPF
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, UlschStrategy=ULSCH_STRATEGY_EPF;
//Enabling normal preallocation and smart preallocation
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=PreAllocationSwitch-1&SmartPreAllocationSwitch-1;
//Enabling QCI-level preallocation
ADD CELLPREALLOCGROUP: LocalCellId=0, PreallocationParaGroupId=0, SmartPreallocationSwitch=ON;
MOD CELLQCIPARA: LocalCellId=0, Qci=9, PreallocationParaGroupId=0;
//Enabling adaptation of the available bandwidth for preallocation
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, PrealloSystemBwCoeff=5;
//Enabling adaptation of the smart preallocation duration
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, SmartPrealloDurationCoeff=6;

Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling

## Optimization Command Examples
//Setting parameters related to normal preallocation and smart preallocation
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, PreAllocationBandwidthRatio=25, PreAllocationMinPeriod=5,
PreAllocationSize=80, SmartPreAllocationDuration=50, PreallocationUserCntThld=50;
MOD QCIPARA: Qci=9, PreAllocationWeight=10;
//Setting parameters related to preallocation of sparse-packet services (ping services)
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, SmartPreAllocDuraForSparse=1500,
PreAllocMinPeriodForSparse=SAME_AS_OTHER_SERVICE,
PreallocationSizeForSparse=SAME_AS_OTHER_SERVICE;
//Setting QCI-level preallocation parameters
MOD CELLPREALLOCGROUP: LocalCellId=0, PreallocationParaGroupId=0, PreallocationMinPeriod=5,
PreallocationSize=80, SmartPreallocationDuration=50;
## Deactivation Command Examples
The following provides only deactivation command examples. You can determine
whether to restore the settings of other parameters based on actual network
conditions.
//Disabling normal preallocation and smart preallocation
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=PreAllocationSwitch-0&SmartPreAllocationSwitch-0;

## 6.5.4.4 Activation Verification
## Using Counters
If the value of L.ChMeas.PRB.UL.PreSch.Used.Avg is not 0 after preallocation is
enabled, preallocation has taken effect.
## Using Monitoring Tasks
Use the MAE-Access to verify whether preallocation has taken effect. Perform the
following steps:
-  Enable a UE to access a cell in the cell center. Do not use the UE to perform
uplink services.
-  On the MAE-Access, start UE-level channel quality monitoring (corresponding
to Quality of Channel Monitoring on the GUI) and MCS-based count
monitoring (corresponding to MCS Count Monitoring on the GUI).
-  Check the results of the two monitoring tasks. The channel quality monitoring
task shows the channel quality for the UE, and the MCS-based count
monitoring task shows the number of times the UE is scheduled in the uplink.
If the SINR is approximately 20 dB, and the UE is scheduled 200 times per
second in the uplink after preallocation is enabled, preallocation has taken
effect. (The number of scheduling times is consistent with the default
preallocation period of 5 ms.)
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.59

## 6.5.4.5 Network Monitoring
For details, see 6.5.2 Network Analysis.
6.6 Outer-loop DSCP-based Preallocation
## 6.6.1 Principles
On the transmission side, operators can use service awareness devices (integrated
into the core network or provided by a third party) to identify services and map
the services to differentiated services code points (DSCPs). DSCPs are classified
into outer-loop DSCPs and inner-loop DSCPs. For details, see
## Video Experience
Optimization. The eNodeB can obtain the outer-loop DSCP value of a service only
when the OUTER_LOOP_DSCP_QOS_MGMT_SW option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
DlSchEnhSwitch parameter is selected. In addition, the
QciPara.OLDscpTransparentTransSw parameter must be set to ON to ensure
that outer-loop DSCP values can be transparently transmitted to the PDCP layer of
the eNodeB.
Based on the obtained outer-loop DSCP values, the eNodeB can perform uplink
preallocation for UEs running specific services. This function requires that both the
DSCP_PREALLOCATION_SW option of the CellAlgoExtSwitch.
UlSchEnhSwitch
parameter and the PREALLOCATION_SW option of the
IpServiceQos.
ApplicationRange parameter be selected. In this case, the data
volume that can be preallocated resources for UEs running such services is
determined by the CellUlschAlgo.
SplUePreallocDataVolume parameter, and the
minimum preallocation interval is determined by the
CellUlschAlgo.
SplUePreallocMinInterval parameter.
Outer-loop DSCP-based preallocation takes effect only when normal preallocation
is enabled. When smart preallocation is enabled, smart preallocation also takes
effect for these UEs for a period specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
SplUeSmartPreallocDuration parameter.
Preallocation for these UEs is not affected by the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreallocationUserCntThld and
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationBandwidthRatio parameters.
●  Preallocation can still take effect for these UEs running specific services when
the number of UEs in RRC_CONNECTED mode in a cell exceeds the value of
the CellUlschAlgo.
PreallocationUserCntThld parameter.
●  If the ratio of the number of preallocated RBs in the current TTI to the
number of RBs in the cell bandwidth exceeds the value of the
CellUlschAlgo.
PreAllocationBandwidthRatio parameter, preallocation can
still take effect for these UEs running specific services.
## 6.6.2 Network Analysis
## 6.6.2.1 Benefits
Outer-loop DSCP-based preallocation increases the number of times the eNodeB
proactively schedules UEs running specific services. This reduces the buffer
duration of uplink data packets for such UEs and improves user experience.
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.60

## 6.6.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 6.6.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
Outer-loop DSCP-based preallocation increases air interface resource consumption,
CCE usage, and uplink RB usage. An increased uplink RB usage results in increased
uplink interference.

6.7 Scheduling Restriction on Abnormal UEs
## 6.7.1 Principles
Scheduling Restriction on UEs with Abnormal Uplink Padding
When a UE sends a large amount of uplink padding data, the MAC layer of the UE
sends a large amount of data, but the throughput at the PDCP layer does not
change significantly. Such a UE is called a UE with abnormal uplink padding.
UEs with abnormal uplink padding preempt a large number of uplink RBs,
affecting the uplink rates of normal UEs. The function of scheduling restriction on
UEs with abnormal uplink padding periodically identifies UEs with abnormal
uplink padding and restricts the number of RBs that can be scheduled for these
UEs. This function is controlled by the ABN_UE_SCH_RESTRICTION_SW option of
the CellAlgoExtSwitch.
UlSchEnhSwitch parameter.
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.64

●Identification of abnormal UEs: Based on UEs' BSRs and proportions of
abnormal padding data, the eNodeB periodically identifies the UEs with
abnormal uplink padding.
–   If the CellUlschAlgo.AbnormalUeSchCntThld parameter is set to 0, the
eNodeB periodically identifies any UE with abnormal uplink padding
based on UEs' PRB usages and proportions of abnormal padding data.
–   If the CellUlschAlgo.AbnormalUeSchCntThld parameter is set to a value
other than 0, the eNodeB checks the expected number of RBs to be
scheduled for a UE and the proportion of abnormal padding data to
identify whether the UE has abnormal padding. If the eNodeB determines
that the padding of a UE is abnormal for a consecutive number of times
specified by the CellUlschAlgo.
AbnormalUeSchCntThld parameter, the
eNodeB identifies the UE as a UE with abnormal uplink padding.
●  Scheduling restriction on abnormal UEs: The eNodeB restricts within a fixed
period the number of RBs that can be scheduled for identified UEs with
abnormal uplink padding. This prevents abnormal UEs from occupying excess
RBs and increases the average uplink data rate of UEs in a cell. If the
CellUlschAlgo.
AbnormalUeSchCntThld parameter is set to a value other
than 0 and the proportion of abnormal padding data of a UE becomes
normal within a fixed period of time, the eNodeB cancels the scheduling
restriction on the UE.
Scheduling Restriction for UEs with Abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs
This function is controlled by the ABN_UE_SCH_RESTRICTION_SW2 option of the
CellAlgoExtSwitch.
UlSchEnhSwitch parameter.
●  Abnormal UE identification: The eNodeB identifies a UE (excluding eMTC UEs)
as one with abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs in any of the following scenarios:
–   The UE continuously reports SRs but has no uplink data.
–   The UE continuously reports SRB BSRs but does not have uplink SRB data.
–   The UE continuously reports SRB BSRs indicating a relatively large buffer
size but has a relatively low proportion of valid uplink SRB data volume
to the total valid uplink data volume.
●  Scheduling restriction on abnormal UEs: The eNodeB restricts the number of
RBs that can be scheduled for identified UEs with abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs
and lowers the scheduling priorities of these UEs.
## NO TE
Given that SRBs belong to logical channel group (LCG) 0, a buffer status report in which
the LCG ID is 0 is referred to as an SRB BSR in this document.
## 6.7.2 Network Analysis
## 6.7.2.1 Benefits
●  Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal uplink padding
Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal uplink padding identifies UEs
with abnormal uplink padding and restricts the number of RBs that can be
scheduled for such UEs, ensuring RB resources for normal UEs and increasing
the average uplink rate of UEs in the cell.
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.65

You are advised to enable this function for cells meeting the following
conditions. If any of the conditions is not met, there will be limited benefits,
but no negative impacts.
–   There is a sharp increase in the uplink PRB usage for a period of time (for
example, half an hour).
–   The average uplink rate of UEs in the cell decreases.
●  Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs
–   If there are abnormal UEs that continuously report SRs but have no
uplink data, this function prevents these UEs from occupying excess RB
resources to increase the average uplink rate of UEs.
–   If there are abnormal UEs that continuously report SRB BSRs but have no
uplink SRB data or that continuously report SRB BSRs indicating a
relatively large buffer size but have a relatively low proportion of valid
uplink SRB data volume to total valid uplink data volume, this function
ensures resource scheduling fairness.
## 6.7.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 6.7.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
This function has the following network impacts:
●  Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal uplink padding
## None
●  Scheduling restriction on UEs with abnormal SRs or SRB BSRs
–   If there are abnormal UEs that continuously report SRs but have no
uplink data, the random access success rate may deteriorate, and
indicators such as the uplink BLER, PRB usage, RB usage, number of
scheduled UEs per TTI (SUNPT), or uplink traffic volume may fluctuate.
–   If there are abnormal UEs that continuously report SRB BSRs but have no
uplink SRB data or that continuously report SRB BSRs indicating a
relatively large buffer size but have a relatively low proportion of valid
uplink SRB data volume to total valid uplink data volume, the uplink
throughput of these UEs decreases and the random access success rate
may deteriorate. In addition, indicators such as the uplink BLER, PRB
usage, RB usage, SUNPT, or uplink traffic volume may fluctuate.

6.8 Preallocation for NSA DC UEs (FDD)
## 6.8.1 Principles
Uplink preallocation is restricted by the number of UEs in RRC_CONNECTED mode
in a cell. In the event of using non-standalone (NSA) networking during LTE-to-5G
evolution, the UE number threshold for performing preallocation for NSA DC UEs
is separately specified by the NsaDcMgmtConfig.
NsaDcUePreUserCntThld
parameter to ensure that uplink preallocation can be performed for more NSA DC
UEs.
●  If this parameter is set to 0 for a cell, preallocation for NSA DC UEs in the cell
is the same as that for non-NSA DC UEs. For details, see 6.5 Preallocation.
●  When this parameter is set to a non-zero value:
–   If the number of UEs in RRC_CONNECTED mode in a cell exceeds the
specified threshold, the eNodeB does not perform preallocation for NSA
DC UEs. For details about preallocation for non-NSA DC UEs, see 6.5
## Preallocation.
–   If the number of UEs in RRC_CONNECTED mode in a cell does not exceed
the threshold, the eNodeB performs preallocation for NSA DC UEs and
non-NSA DC UEs. For details, see 6.5 Preallocation. However,
preallocation for NSA DC UEs has the following differences:
## ▪
The cell-level common preallocation switch and the UE number
threshold for preallocation do not take effect for NSA DC UEs.
Specifically, when the SmartPreAllocationSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is selected, smart
preallocation is performed for NSA DC UEs. If this option is
deselected, normal preallocation is performed for NSA DC UEs.
▪When normal preallocation takes effect for UEs in DRX state, normal
preallocation in the On Duration period can be performed for NSA
DC UEs but is not available for non-NSA DC UEs unless the
CellDrxPara.
DrxOdPreSchSwitch parameter is set to ON.
## 6.8.2 Network Analysis
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.69

## 6.8.2.1 Benefits
By increasing the number of times that the base station proactively schedules UEs,
preallocation for NSA DC UEs reduces the uplink data packet buffering time in the
UEs, increases the response speed for UE services, and improves the service
experience.
## 6.8.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 6.8.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
The function has no impact on the network.

//Setting the UE number threshold in preallocation for NSA DC UEs to a non-zero value

The following provides only deactivation command examples. You can determine
whether to restore the settings of other parameters based on actual network
conditions.

MOD NSADCMGMTCONFIG: LocalCellId=0, NsaDcUePreUserCntThld=0;
//Enabling normal preallocation for NSA DC UEs
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=SmartPreAllocationSwitch-0;

6.9 Uplink Short-Interval SPS (FDD)
## 6.9.1 Principles
A UE must obtain an uplink grant before it can transmit data in the uplink. If a
relatively long time is required to obtain the uplink grant, uplink short-interval SPS
can be used for UEs that are running services requiring low latency to reduce the
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description6 UE Selection for Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.72

UE transmission delay for the first uplink packet. Services with a specific QCI
require low latency if the CellQciPara.LowLatencyFlag parameter is set to TRUE
for this QCI.
The interval of uplink short-interval SPS is specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.IntvlOfUlSpsWithSkipping parameter.
●  When this parameter is set to a value other than MS0, short-interval SPS is
performed at the specified interval using the TBS specified by the
CellUlschAlgo.
TbsForUlSpsWithSkipping parameter.
●  When this parameter is set to MS0, the function does not take effect.
## 6.9.2 Network Analysis
## 6.9.2.1 Benefits
In uplink light load scenarios (for example, the number of UEs is less than 100 and
the PUSCH RB usage is less than 30%), uplink short-interval SPS reduces the
transmission delay for the first uplink packet of related UEs.
## 6.9.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 6.9.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
This function has the following network impacts:
●  UEs that have entered uplink short-interval SPS occupy fixed uplink resources.
This increases the uplink RB usage and decreases the number of uplink RBs
for other UEs. As a result, Cell Uplink Average Throughput decreases.
●  Uplink short-interval SPS for some UEs postpones the uplink feedback to
downlink services of other UEs. As a result, Cell Downlink Average
Throughput decreases.
●  When UEs that have entered uplink short-interval SPS suddenly move to a
weak coverage area, the eNodeB may fail to identify whether the UEs are in
the skipping state or uplink data transmission state. As a result, Service Drop
Rate increases.

7 UE Service Priorities in Uplink
## Scheduling
Schedulers use the EPF scheduling algorithm to meet end-to-end QoS
requirements. Unlike downlink QoS guarantee, uplink QoS is guaranteed by using
related parameters on the eNodeB and related parameters sent from the eNodeB
to UEs. The eNodeB can obtain accurate information about the data volumes of
downlink services and perform downlink scheduling based on the data volumes
and service priorities. However, the eNodeB cannot obtain accurate information
about the data volumes of uplink services. Therefore, the eNodeB performs uplink
scheduling based on the scheduling priority of each UE. The uplink services of
each scheduled UE are then scheduled based on their data volumes and service
priorities.
UE services can be classified as either GBR services or non-GBR services. When
both GBR and non-GBR services are running in a cell, the scheduler preferentially
guarantees the rates of GBR services, and adopts a best-effort policy for non-GBR
services with a minimum bit rate guaranteed. Scenario-specific conditions must
also be considered for scheduling priority calculation and service rate guarantee.
This section describes the following related functions:
●  Uplink GBR and non-GBR service priority calculation
●  Uplink scheduling based on the maximum bit rate
●  MBR-based uplink rate control
●  Uplink traffic-model-based performance optimization
●  Optimization of uplink scheduling priority accuracy
●  Uplink scheduling duration optimization
●  Rate Guarantee Optimization for Uplink Non-GBR Services
7.1 Uplink GBR and Non-GBR Service Priority
## Calculation
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description7 UE Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.81

## 7.1.1 Principles
UE Parameters for Uplink QoS
Uplink QoS is guaranteed using related parameters on the eNodeB and those that
the eNodeB sends to the UEs. The parameters for uplink QoS on the UE side are
as follows:
●  Prioritized bit rate (PBR)
In accordance with 3GPP specifications, a PBR must be preferentially ensured
on uplink logical channels. The PBR ensures that UEs on low-priority logical
channels are scheduled when resource congestion occurs, preventing the
service rate from dropping to zero on such channels due to resource
congestion. The value range of the PBR for logical channels is specified in
section 6.3.2 in 3GPP TS 36.331 V10.1.0 (2011-03).
Table 7-1 lists the PBR setting principles for GBR services and non-GBR
services.
Table 7-1 PBR setting principles
Logical Channel TypePBR Setting
Dedicated control channel (DCCH)
(SRB1 and SRB2)
## Infinity
QCI 1, QCI 65, and QCI 6616 KB/s
QCI 5 and QCI 698 KB/s
QCI 2 to QCI 4Configurable using the
QciPara.PrioritisedBitRate parameter
QCI 6 to QCI 9
Other QCIs

●  Logical channel priority
Logical channels carry services for UEs. Services with different QCIs can be
differentiated by setting the QciPara.LogicalChannelPriority parameter.
The recommended logical channel priorities for services with different QCIs

The configuration suggestions and details are as follows:
–   A larger value for the logical channel priority indicates a lower scheduling
priority.
–   To meet the QoS requirements of GBR services and signaling with QCI 5:
## ▪
Set the logical channel priority for GBR services and signaling with
QCI 5 to a value from 6 to 10.
▪Set the logical channel priority for non-GBR services to a value from
11 to 15.
–   Non-GBR services other than non-GBR PTT services can be classified as
follows:
▪High-priority non-GBR services, whose logical channel priority is 11,
12, or 13.
▪Low-priority non-GBR services, whose logical channel priority is 14,
15, or 16.
●  Logical channel group
A UE can report the total amount of buffered data for multiple services with
different QCIs, but not for each individual service. This reduces consumption

Non-GBR Service Priority Calculation
The uplink scheduler uses a best-effort policy for non-GBR service transmission,
and ensures that the total rate of all non-GBR services of a UE does not exceed
the AMBR configured in the EPC. When the UlMinGbrSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is selected, the eNodeB can identify UEs
by QCI or SPID and set an uplink minimum GBR for specific UEs.
●  QCI-based identification: Operators can specify different QCIs for default non-
GBR bearers of different UE types on the EPC. An uplink minimum GBR for
bearers with different QCIs can be configured using the
CellQciParaExtension.
UlMinGbr parameter on the eNodeB.
●  SPID-based identification: Operators can specify different SPIDs for different
UE types on the EPC. An uplink minimum GBR corresponding to the default
non-GBR bearers of UEs with a specified SPID can be configured using the
SpidCfg.
UlMinGbr parameter on the eNodeB.
For a specific UE, the eNodeB uses the value of the SpidCfg.
UlMinGbr parameter
as the uplink minimum GBR if it is a non-zero value; otherwise, the eNodeB uses
the value of the CellQciParaExtension.
UlMinGbr parameter.
●  When Uu resources are sufficient:
–   If the minimum GBR of non-GBR services does not exceed the PBR, the
service rate can reach the minimum GBR.
–   If the minimum GBR of non-GBR services exceeds the PBR, the service
rate may not reach the minimum GBR when multiple bearers are set up,
especially for low-priority non-GBR services. This is because UEs
guarantee the PBRs in descending order of logical-channel priorities.
●  When Uu resources are insufficient:
–   If both GBR and non-GBR services are running in the cell, GBR services
are preferentially scheduled. This means that the non-GBR service rates
may not reach the minimum GBR.
–   If only non-GBR services are running in the cell and the amount of uplink
resources required to meet the minimum GBR of a UE exceeds the
amount of resources provided by the system, the non-GBR service rate of
the UE may not reach its minimum GBR.
The eNodeB uses a token bucket algorithm to control the uplink service rate. The
size of the token bucket affects the guaranteed rate for services and the service
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description7 UE Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.86

rate determines the rate at which tokens are added to the bucket. The number of
tokens in the bucket indicates whether the service rate meets requirements.
The priority is then calculated by using the following formula:
where
● indicates the current channel quality of the UE.
● indicates the historical transmission rate of the UE.
## ●
indicates the QCI-specific weight factor for the scheduling priority of the
service. This factor can be set using the
CellQciParaExtension.UlSchPriorityWeightFactor parameter. A larger weight
factor indicates a higher scheduling priority.
## ●
indicates the weight coefficient for the uplink scheduling priority
corresponding to the SPID of the UE. This weight coefficient is specified by the
SpidCfg.
UlSchPriCoeff parameter and is valid only when the eNodeB
identifies that the UE is configured with an SPID. For details, see WBB.
● indicates the capacity factor for EPF. This factor can be set using the
CellUlschAlgo.
UlEpfCapacityFactor parameter.
–   When the factor is set to 1, the scheduling priority selection is oriented
towards resource fairness.
–   When the factor is set to a value smaller than 1, the scheduling priority
selection is oriented towards rate fairness.
–   When the factor is set to a value greater than 1, the scheduling priority
selection is oriented towards capacity.
GBR Service Priority Calculation
The uplink scheduler considers the channel quality and historical transmission rate
of a UE when calculating the GBR service priority for the UE and preferentially
guarantees the rate of GBR services.
## 7.1.2 Network Analysis
## 7.1.2.1 Benefits
GBR and non-GBR service priority calculation guarantees the QoS for services of
various QCIs and provides differentiated services.
## 7.1.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 7.1.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
In the priority configuration of logical channels from QCI 1 to QCI 5:
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description7 UE Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.87

●  If the logical channel priority of QCI 1 is lower than that of QCI 2 to QCI 4,
the Uplink Packet Loss Rate (VoIP) may increase in hybrid services of QCI 1
and QCI 2 to QCI 4.
●  If the logical channel priority of QCI 1 is higher than that of QCI 5, the
scheduling delay for QCI-5 bearers may slightly increase in multi-bearer
service scenarios with QCI 1 and QCI 5.
●  If the logical channel priority of QCI 1 is higher than that of QCI 5 and the
preallocation weight for QCI 1 is 0 (indicating that preallocation is disabled
when the bearer for QCI 1 is available), the Uplink Packet Loss Rate (VoIP)
may increase due to missing detection of SRs.
In minimum GBR configuration for non-GBR services:
●  Given that the eNodeB preferentially guarantees the rates of UEs configured
with minimum GBR, available resources decrease for non-GBR UEs without
such configuration, possibly causing Service Uplink Average Throughput to
decrease for such UEs.
●  When most UEs configured with uplink minimum GBR are located at the cell
edge, the eNodeB uses excessive resources to guarantee the rates of these
UEs, possibly causing Cell Uplink Average Throughput to decrease.
●  When most UEs configured with uplink minimum GBR are located near the
cell center, the eNodeB uses excessive resources to guarantee the rates of
these UEs, possibly causing Cell Uplink Average Throughput to increase.

Before using MML commands, refer to 7.1.3 Requirements and complete the
parameter configurations for related functions based on the impact, dependency,
and mutually exclusive relationships between the functions, as well as the actual
network scenario. In addition, ensure that the license, hardware, networking, and
cell requirements are met.
Before using MML commands, confirm the related parameter modification
precautions by referring to the fields including "Service Interrupted After
Modification" and "Caution" for the corresponding parameters in the parameter
reference.
## Activation Command Examples
//Selecting the UlMinGbrSwitch option
MOD CELLALGOSWITCH: LocalCellId=0, UlSchSwitch=UlMinGbrSwitch-1;
## Optimization Command Examples
//Setting the uplink minimum guaranteed rate
MOD CELLQCIPARAEXTENSION: LocalCellId=0, Qci=6, UlMinGbr=MinGbrRate_1_KB;
MOD SPIDCFG: Spid=10, UlMinGbr=MinGbrRate_1_KB;
//Setting parameters related to logical channels
MOD GLOBALPROCSWITCH: LcgProfile=LCG_PROFILE_0;
MOD QCIPARA: Qci=6, PrioritisedBitRate=PBR_8_KBps, LogicalChannelPriority=12;
//Configuring the capacity factor for uplink EPF scheduling
MOD CELLULSCHALGO: LocalCellId=0, UlEpfCapacityFactor=EPF_CAPC_FACTOR_1;
//Configuring the uplink scheduling priority weight factor
MOD CELLQCIPARAEXTENSION: LocalCellId=0, Qci=6, UlSchPriorityWeightFactor=950;

## 7.1.4.4 Activation Verification
The following example shows how to observe whether the uplink minimum GBR
of UEs takes effect when the UlMinGbrSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is selected.
-  Enable two cell center UEs (UE1 and UE2) with the same channel conditions
to access a 5 MHz cell and set up non-GBR bearers. The bearer of UE 1 uses
QCI 8, with CellQciParaExtension.
UlMinGbr set to MinGbrRate_1536_KB.
The bearer of UE 2 uses QCI 9, with CellQciParaExtension.UlMinGbr set to
MinGbrRate_0_KB.
2.Confirm that the SchedulerCtrlPowerSwitch option of the
CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is deselected. Then, perform uplink
full buffer services for the two UEs at the same time.
-  Check the UL Used RB Num values for the two UEs on the MAE-Access to
obtain the number of RBs used by each UE before and after the
UlMinGbrSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter is
selected. Before the option is selected, the ratio between the numbers of RBs
used by the two UEs is close to 1:1. After the option is selected, the
percentage of RBs used by UE 1 increases, indicating that this function has
taken effect.

7.2 Uplink Scheduling Based on the Maximum Bit Rate
## 7.2.1 Principles
In the EPC, a UE-AMBR is set for the total rate of all uplink non-GBR services and
an MBR is set for the total rate of all uplink GBR services for each UE.
When different UEs in a cell have different UE-AMBRs:
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description7 UE Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.91

●  If the UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch
parameter is deselected, the eNodeB does not consider UE-AMBRs when
calculating scheduling priorities.
●  If the UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.UlSchSwitch
parameter is selected, the eNodeB considers UE-AMBRs when calculating
scheduling priorities to implement differentiated scheduling of non-GBR
services with different service rate subscriptions.
When different UEs in a cell have different MBRs:
●  If the MbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter
is deselected, the eNodeB does not consider MBRs when calculating
scheduling priorities.
●  If the MbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter
is selected, the eNodeB considers MBRs when calculating scheduling priorities
to implement differentiated scheduling of GBR services with different service
rate subscriptions.
## 7.2.2 Network Analysis
## 7.2.2.1 Benefits
●  When the UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option is selected, UEs with high UE-AMBRs
are preferentially allocated resources during scheduling. The average uplink
UE throughput increases for such UEs.
●  When the MbrUlSchSwitch option is selected, UEs with high MBRs are
preferentially allocated resources during scheduling. The average uplink UE
throughput increases for such UEs.
## 7.2.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 7.2.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
This function has the following network impacts:
●  In the case of congestion, selecting the UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option causes
UEs with high AMBRs to occupy a large amount of resources. This may reduce
## Cell Uplink Average Throughput.
●  In the case of congestion, selecting the MbrUlSchSwitch option causes UEs
with high MBRs to occupy a large amount of resources. This may reduce Cell
## Uplink Average Throughput.

## 7.2.4.4 Activation Verification
-  Enable UE 1, UE 2, and UE 3 with the same channel conditions and an AMBR
of 20 Mbit/s, 40 Mbit/s, and 40 Mbit/s, respectively, to access a 10 MHz cell
from the cell center and have non-GBR bearers established.

UeAmbrUlSchSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch parameter

RBs used by each UE is almost the same. If the percentage of RBs used by UE
1 decreases after this option is selected, this function has taken effect.
-  Verify the function activation on UEs running GBR services in a similar way.

7.3 MBR-based Uplink Rate Control
## 7.3.1 Principles
The MBR is a QoS parameter set in the EPC for GBR services. Generally, the MBR
must be greater than or equal to the GBR. When the scheduler schedules GBR
services, it determines whether to consider the MBR for uplink rate control based
on the setting of the UlMbrCtrlSwitch option of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchSwitch
parameter.
●  If the UlMbrCtrlSwitch option is deselected, the uplink scheduler uses a best-
effort policy to allocate air interface resources to ensure that the total rate of
GBR services on the UE reaches but does not exceed the total GBR of all GBR
services.
●  If the UlMbrCtrlSwitch option is selected, the uplink scheduler uses a best-
effort policy to allocate air interface resources to ensure that the total rate of
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description7 UE Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.95

GBR services on the UE reaches but does not exceed the total MBR of all GBR
services.
## 7.3.2 Network Analysis
## 7.3.2.1 Benefits
If the UlMbrCtrlSwitch option is selected, GBR services can be allocated more
resources. The average uplink service rate of GBR services increases and can
exceed the GBR, with the MBR as the upper limit.
## 7.3.2.2 Impacts
The impacts between this function and other functions are described in 7.3.3.2.3
## Function Impacts.
When the system is congested, GBR bearers consume more resources, which in
return reduces the resources available for non-GBR bearers. As a result, the
average uplink throughput on the non-GBR bearers decreases.

7.4 Uplink Traffic-Model-based Performance
## Optimization
## 7.4.1 Principles
On live networks, traffic models differ greatly depending on the UEs. The
scheduler considers traffic models as well as signal quality during scheduling. It
preferentially schedules UEs with smaller amount of service buffer data and better
signal quality. This function is controlled by the UlPacketLenAwareSchSw option
of the CellAlgoSwitch.
UlSchExtSwitch parameter.
## 7.4.2 Network Analysis
## 7.4.2.1 Benefits
In scenarios with a heavy traffic load (for example, with the number of UEs
greater than 150 and the PUSCH RB usage greater than 60%), uplink traffic-
model-based scheduling reduces service wait time for UEs, allows higher-order
uplink MCSs, and increases average uplink user-perceived throughput by 5% to
20% while basically maintaining cell capacity. In scenarios with a light traffic load,
this function increases average uplink user-perceived throughput less significantly
but does not bring additional negative gains. (Average uplink user-perceived
throughput = L.Thrp.bits.UE.UL.Experienced/L.Thrp.Time.UE.UL.Experienced)
eRAN
Uplink Scheduling Feature Parameter Description7 UE Service Priorities in Uplink Scheduling
Issue 03 (2025-06-28)Copyright © Huawei Technologies Co., Ltd.98

## Function Impacts.
